This is\n22.  * horrible, but the geotools way of doing things is too slow.\n23.  *\n24.  * @author novalis\n25.  */\n26. public class UnifiedGridCoverage extends AbstractCoverage {\n27. \n28.   /**\n29.    * A spatial index of the intersection of all regions and datums. For smaller-scale deployments,\n30.    * this spatial index might perform more slowly than manually iterating over each region and\n31.    * datum. However, in larger regions, this can result in 20+% more calculations being done during\n32.    * the same time compared to the brute-force method. In smaller-scale deployments since the\n33.    * overall time is not as long, we leave this in here for the benefit of larger regions where this\n34.    * will result in much better performance.\n35.    */\n36.   private final SpatialIndex datumRegionIndex;\n37.   private final ArrayList<Coverage> regions;\n38. \n39.   /**\n40.    * It would be nice if we could construct this unified coverage with zero sub-coverages and add\n41.    * all sub-coverages in the same way. However, the superclass constructor (AbstractCoverage) needs\n42.    * a coverage to copy properties from. So the first sub-coverage needs to be passed in at\n43.    * construction time.\n44.    */\n45.   protected UnifiedGridCoverage(List<GridCoverage2D> regionCoverages, List<VerticalDatum> datums) {\n46.     super(\"unified\", regionCoverages.get(0));\n47.     regions = new ArrayList<>();\n48.     datumRegionIndex = new STRtree();\n49. \n50.     // Iterate through region coverages, creating Interpolators for each region and then adding them and the\n51.     // intersected datum to the spatial index.\n52.     for (GridCoverage2D regionCoverage : regionCoverages) {\n53.       // TODO might bicubic interpolation give better results?\n54.       GridCoverage2D regionCoverageInterpolator = Interpolator2D.create(\n55.         regionCoverage,\n56.         new InterpolationBilinear()\n57.       );\n58. \n59.       // Iterate over datums to find intersection envelope with each region and add to spatial index.\n60.       for (VerticalDatum datum : datums) {\n61.         Envelope datumEnvelope = new Envelope(\n62.           datum.lowerLeftLongitude,\n63.           datum.lowerLeftLongitude + datum.deltaLongitude,\n64.           datum.lowerLeftLatitude,\n65.           datum.lowerLeftLatitude + datum.deltaLatitude\n66.         );\n67.         ReferencedEnvelope regionEnvelope = new ReferencedEnvelope(\n68.           regionCoverageInterpolator.getEnvelope()\n69.         );\n70.         Envelope intersection = regionEnvelope.intersection(datumEnvelope);\n71.         datumRegionIndex.insert(intersection, new DatumRegion(datum, regionCoverageInterpolator));\n72.       }\n73.       regions.add(regionCoverageInterpolator);\n74.     }\n75.   }\n76. \n77.   @Override\n78.   public Object evaluate(DirectPosition point) throws CannotEvaluateException {\n79.     /* we don't use this function, we use evaluate(DirectPosition point, double[] values) */\n80.     return null;\n81.   }\n82. \n83.   @Override\n84.   public int getNumSampleDimensions() {\n85.     return regions.get(0).getNumSampleDimensions();\n86.   }\n87. \n88.   @Override\n89.   public SampleDimension getSampleDimension(int index) throws IndexOutOfBoundsException {\n90.     return regions.get(0).getSampleDimension(index);\n91.   }\n92. \n93.   /**\n94.    * Calculate the elevation at a given point\n95.    */\n96.   public double[] evaluate(DirectPosition point, double[] values) throws CannotEvaluateException {\n97.     double x = point.getOrdinate(0);\n98.     double y = point.getOrdinate(1);\n99.     Coordinate pointCoordinate = new Coordinate(x, y);\n100.     Envelope envelope = new Envelope(pointCoordinate);\n101. This is\n  * horrible, but the geotools way of doing things is too slow.\n  *\n  * @author novalis\n  */\n public class UnifiedGridCoverage extends AbstractCoverage {\n \n   /**\n    * A spatial index of the intersection of all regions and datums. For smaller-scale deployments,\n    * this spatial index might perform more slowly than manually iterating over each region and\n    * datum. However, in larger regions, this can result in 20+% more calculations being done during\n    * the same time compared to the brute-force method. In smaller-scale deployments since the\n    * overall time is not as long, we leave this in here for the benefit of larger regions where this\n    * will result in much better performance.\n    */\n   private final SpatialIndex datumRegionIndex;\n   private final ArrayList<Coverage> regions;\n \n   /**\n    * It would be nice if we could construct this unified coverage with zero sub-coverages and add\n    * all sub-coverages in the same way. super(\"unified\", regionCoverages.get(0));\n     regions = new ArrayList<>();\n     datumRegionIndex = new STRtree();\n \n     // Iterate through region coverages, creating Interpolators for each regionEnvelope.intersection(datumEnvelope);\n         datumRegionIndex.insert(intersection, new DatumRegion(datum, regionCoverageInterpolator));\n       }\n       regions.add(regionCoverageInterpolator);\n     }\n   }\n \n   @Override\n   public Object evaluate(DirectPosition point) throws CannotEvaluateException {\n     /* we don't use this function, we use evaluate(DirectPosition point, double[] values) */\n     return null;\n   }\n \n   @Override\n   public int getNumSampleDimensions() {\n     return regions.get(0).getNumSampleDimensions();\n   }\n \n
